# Batch Summaries

**A rolling file holds the open volume, the two previous batches and the standing locks; a closed volume, and the third batch back, is read in `state/archive/`. This file runs oldest-first, so the newest record is at the BOTTOM. Volume 12 is closed and so are Volumes 10 and 11, and there is no open volume: this file holds Volume 12's own record and its standing locks.**

**What is in this file is Volume 12's measured record, the sections for Batches 0001 to 0007 and for the repairs that followed four of them, plus the Volume 12 close.** The per-batch records of Volume 12's Batches 0001 to 0004 and the whole of the Volume 10 and Volume 11 record are in `state/archive/`. Every measured figure in this project lives in this file and in no other rolling file; the two exceptions are the canon at the volume boundaries in `state/continuity.md` and the protagonist's floor table in `state/archive/volume-12-close.md`.

**The entry format below is the format.** It is kept because a writer inherits it, and a record written in a shape its successor does not expect is a record that gets written wrongly.

# THE REPAIR OF VOLUME 12 BATCH 0003, AND WHAT IT DID AND DID NOT DO — A REPAIR AND NOT A BATCH, AND IT MEASURED NO CHAPTER

*Written 2026-09-30 by the phase that took the reviewer findings at `logs/batch-0003.review.log`. **A repair and not a batch, not a close, not an outline, not a review and not a second reading.** It opened no chapter of any volume, changed no date and no event and no planned plot, and resolved nothing. **This section prints almost no figure, and that is the honest shape of a repair whose findings were all about a phase boundary and not about a page: a repair that measured the page would be manufacturing the appearance of a batch, and the one figure class it does print is the line count of the file it repaired and the existence of the prompt it wrote, because those are the two things it changed.** It wrote one prompt, amended one header and ten bullets in `state/current.md`, appended this section, added item 158 to `state/open-threads.md`, and added one line to one retired prompt. It edited nothing under `scripts/`, `.github/workflows/` or `.opencode/agent/`, and it did not touch `state/phase-ledger.json`, and it wrote no marker of any kind in any phase directory.

## One: the six findings, and what happened to each one, in the order the review gave them

**Finding 1, blocking, correct, and the whole of the repair.** The required next-phase prompt was never created. **`workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` now exists and is 43,362 bytes and 8,040 words, measured with `wc -c` and `wc -w`; 43,340 of those bytes are characters and the difference is the em dashes and the multiplication sign, and a byte count printed as a character count is a copy of a figure with a new label on it.** It is the prompt for Movement Four, Chapters 0581 to 0590, and it is the only next phase. **It is longer than the prompt for the batch before it at 32,926 bytes, and shorter than the one at `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/` that was deferred four times and then rescued, which is 50,663; the length of a prompt is not a finding and no instrument in this repository measures one, and the two figures are printed because the next writer inherits both of them and will wonder which end of the range it is at.**

**And the cause is the finding the review did not make, and it is here because a repair that records only what it was told has not finished.** The batch's prompt carried the instruction to create it, in its last line, and the batch did not do it. **The same file carried a second instruction at its head, prepended by `scripts/rescue_overscoped.py`, whose item three reads *Do not create a next-phase prompt. The pipeline creates it.* Two opposite instructions in one file, and the one at the head won because a block headed `# SCOPE OF THIS RUN - READ FIRST` is the first thing a writer reads.** The header's claim is false about this repository and the batch's own instruction was true, and the writer obeying the head of the file did the right thing given what it was told. **The new prompt resolves the contradiction in its own section Zero, which is the first section after the title, and it does it by arithmetic rather than by assertion: it tells the writer to read the mechanism in `scripts/novel_runner.sh` and see that `ensure_next_phase` returns early whenever any other phase directory is incomplete, and that what it writes when it does fire is a generic continuation stub.** This is item 158 and it is a standing, not a note.

**Finding 2, blocking, correct, and it is the one that was still armed when this repair started.** Both remaining selectable phases were already-finished work. **`workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/` is this phase and the pipeline marks it itself at `scripts/novel_runner.sh:401` when this run ends; `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/` was the armed one, its work is finished prose in a closed volume, and its `.retry-after` had expired, and `find workspace -name PROMPT.md | sort` puts it ahead of every Volume 12 phase.** Method: the reviewer ran the selector's own logic; this repair re-ran the workflow's `scan()` at `novels.yml:133-179` over the tree after the change, and the result is one skip and two selectables, printed in the record below. **The fix is one line at the head of that prompt and nothing else, because `retire_obsolete_phases()` at `novel_runner.sh:43-53` reads `^Retired .*phase` out of a prompt and writes the marker itself, and the workflow's scan honours the same line at `novels.yml:145-147` without writing anything. No marker was written by hand and the prompt body below the new line is byte-identical to what it was.** The ten finished chapters of Volume 11 were not opened.

**Finding 3, correct, and it is not this phase's to write.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/` carried a `.wip-conflict` and no `.done`. The reviewer is right that the fix is controller-side and right about the reason: the marker is written by the runner after the fix pass, and a phase that writes its own `.done` has certified itself. **Carried, named, not repaired, and it is wrong only for the duration of the window this repair is running inside.** The `.wip-conflict` file is controller-written at `novel_runner.sh:157` and is removed by the same pass at line 402.

**Finding 4, correct, and repaired in the file the review named.** `state/current.md`'s header contradicted its own first bullet. **Both are now true and both are now Volume 12 at Chapter 0580.** The first bullet pointed at Volume 12's Batch 0001 as the only next phase; it now points at `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, and the words it carried are named in the bullet. **The bullet has been wrong twice in the same clause — the review repair of the Volume 12 outline fixed it once and the batch phases left it wrong for three batches — and that is named in place.** Five further bullets in the ten lines were a batch behind and are corrected: the continuity pointers, the rooms, the read order, the "nobody was told" standing, the question-mark count, and the measurements pointer. **Every superseded pointer is corrected in place with the words it carried named beside it, and this is the one exception the file's convention makes and the reason it makes it is that a hand-off is a working surface and the archive is where everything below the header goes.** The header's own false claim, that the pipeline creates the next prompt, is corrected in the same pass and the correction carries the line number of the mechanism that refutes it.

**Finding 5, correct as a finding and not a defect in the tree.** `state/current.md` was truncated from 204 lines to 1 by a bad write inside the batch's own run and recovered from the last commit. **It is whole, it stood at 204 lines when the review was taken, and it stands at 236 after this repair appended its record, and the class is item 157's neighbour.** The standing it produces is printed in the new prompt's section Seven: write a state file to a temporary name, read it back, move it over the file you meant to change, and run `git diff --stat` before closing, **because that is the method that found the fault and it is not an instrument in this repository.**

**Finding 6, correct, and carried whole.** `state/phase-ledger.json` reads `phase-000-bootstrap`, `planned`, `attempts: 0`, `range: null` against 580 chapters. **It is a controller file, no phase may edit it, and it is carried and not repaired. What is added here is the reviewer's own observation confirmed by reading both selectors: `PHASE_SYSTEM.md:187` says the selector picks the next batch by reading this ledger, and neither `novel_runner.sh` nor `novels.yml` opens the ledger anywhere.** A ledger that nothing reads cannot be stale in a way that matters, and the documented mechanism and the actual mechanism disagree in a way a reader of the documentation would not guess.

**The two minor items are carried as measurements and neither is a defect.** A title at exactly twenty-five words against a rule reading *under about twenty-five*, and a sentence maximum of eighty-eight at the top of the eighty-five-to-eighty-eight ceiling. **The new prompt asks for twenty-four words and for headroom under eighty, so the next batch has margin; the page is not changed, because a reviewer measured it clean and *about* absorbs twenty-five.**

## Two: the selector, re-run after the change, and the set it was taken over

**Method: the workflow's own `scan()` body from `.github/workflows/novels.yml:133-179`, run by hand over `find workspace -name PROMPT.md -type f | sort`, with the full set it saw printed rather than a summary of it.**

```
SKIP (retired-in-prompt): workspace/volume-11/batch-0003
SELECTABLE: workspace/volume-12/batch-0003
SELECTABLE: workspace/volume-12/batch-0004
```

**One skip, two selectables, out of ninety-four prompt files, and eighty-nine of them were skipped on a `.done` or a `.retired` before the retirement line was read at all.** *The blind spot beside this figure is the phase this repair is running inside: `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/` is selectable for as long as this run lasts, because the runner writes its `.done` at `novel_runner.sh:401` after the fix pass ends and not before, and a scan run from inside the phase always reports the phase it is standing in. That is not a second armed phase and it is not this repair's to close.*

**And the check that the retirement line is the intended one and has not caught anything else: the same `grep -qiE '^Retired .*phase'` over all ninety-four prompt files returns exactly one match, and it is `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/`.** A line that retires a phase is a line that can retire the wrong phase, and the count of the matches is the figure that says it did not.

## Three: what this repair did not do, and the debts

It wrote no chapter and opened no chapter for edit. It did not move Volume 12's Batch 0001 record to `state/archive/`, which is the unmet bound at item 157 and which the next batch's prompt re-states. It wrote no marker in any phase directory. It edited no controller file. It did not touch the four debts, and it could not: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's Batch 0003, **which is four and not three**, and the review gate, which fell back to the writing agent on this batch as it has on every batch phase the commit history records, **and which has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either.** **The number of debts has not moved in eleven volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and this repair wrote one prompt and one retired line and amended one hand-off and discharged nothing. Nothing in this project's records can be certified independent, and that sentence is the last thing a repair is allowed to say about itself.**

# A RUN DISPATCHED ONTO VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0004 AFTER THAT BATCH WAS FINISHED, ON 2026-09-30 — WHAT IT FOUND, WHAT IT CHANGED, AND THE FIGURES IT RE-DERIVED

**This is not a batch, not a repair, not a close, not a review and not a second reading, and it wrote no chapter. It opened no chapter for edit and it changed no line of the novel.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` was taken and its ten chapters were written and its six state sections were written and its next-phase prompt was written, and this run was dispatched onto that finished batch a second time. The honest shape of the run is that it verified finished work and found one real defect, and the defect was in a hand-off and not on a page.

**What was already on the tree when this run opened it, and none of it was written here.** `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0581.md` through `chapter-0590.md` are present and are finished prose, 882 to 1,117 words each. One section for this batch stands at the foot of each of `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and this file. The bound at item 157 is discharged and the block it names is in `state/archive/`. `workspace/volume-12/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` exists, is 160 lines, carries all ten of its sections, and covers Chapters 0591 to 0600. **No second next phase was created and no marker of any kind was written in `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/`; `.done` belongs to the pipeline and a phase that marks its own directory has certified itself.**

## One, the figures the batch's own record published, re-derived by this run, with the method named before each

**Every figure below was taken again and not inherited. Where this run's reading and the batch's record agree, both stand and neither is a copy of the other.**

**Words, `wc -w` one file at a time:** 1,117, 1,040, 1,094, 1,037, 986, 974, 928, 882, 905, 960, and a batch total of **9,923**, which is the figure the batch's own section One prints. `python3 tools/measure.py words` returns **42,536 for Volume 12** across forty files and **1,352,457 for the manuscript in 590 files**.

**Sentence maxima, `python3 tools/measure.py sentences --volume 12`:** Volume 12 at 1,300 sentences, mean 31.823, median 30, maximum 88 in `chapter-0572.md`, which is a closed chapter of Batch 0003. The ten per-file maxima for this batch are **71, 76, 61, 64, 73, 74, 70, 70, 71 and 78** by chapter order, which is the figure the batch's own section Three prints, figure for figure. *This run first measured these with an ad-hoc splitter written in the session and got 95, 114, 66, 65, 74, 83, 94, 103, 72 and 78, which is wrong in four files by twenty-nine words and more. The ad-hoc splitter split on a bare `[.!?]` followed by whitespace and did not carry a closing quotation mark across the boundary, so it joined a closing line to the dialogue that follows it. The repository's splitter is the one that allows a closing mark between the full stop and the space and it is the one the plant at `tools/measure.py` plant 8 exists for. The wrong figures are named here and are not the figures.*

**Calendar, `grep -o "the year after" file | wc -l` one file at a time:** 8 on `chapter-0580.md` and on Chapters 0581 to 0586, and **9 on Chapters 0587 to 0590**. A year name turned inside this batch at Chapter 0587 and this run confirmed the turn against the page rather than against the record. *The blind spot is the batch's and it travels: the count is right and the derivation is not checked by it.*

**`python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 12`:** forty files read, forty parsed date lines, **ten month-boundary restarts of the cycle, zero breaks inside a month**, nothing unparsed. The instrument's ten restarts are the false breaks the prompt warns about and they are not breaks. *Its blind spot is unchanged and it is the one that matters on a batch where a year turns: it groups by the ordinal word and does not read the year at all, so it printed month eleven, month twelve and month one and could not tell that two different named years are in the forty files it read. The zero breaks is a fact about forty files and not a claim that the year did not turn.*

**Question marks, `grep -c '?'` one file at a time: zero in all ten files.** The two figures are independent and both are recorded here.

**Month and weekday sweep at a word boundary over the twelve month names and the seven day names: nothing in any of the ten files.**

**Spent objects, grepped across the ten: no lamp lit, no coat pocket, nothing cut into anything, nobody sent.**

## Two, the re-print check, and the two faults in the detector this run wrote to run it

**This run wrote a detector outside the repository at `/tmp/opencode/rep_12b4.py`, and the prior run's detector was not on the disk it was given, which is the reason and not an excuse.** It aligns by token value and not by index, extends every match to its full maximal length, compares every file against every other file, filters date-line spans by character span before tokenising, and de-duplicates by dominated position.

**And it was planted before it was believed, and it failed its first plant, and the fault was mine and not the instrument's.** The first form carried a line reading `runs(None, tb, n) and [...]`, where the helper returns an empty list for a miss and an empty list is falsy, so the `and` short-circuited and the real search never ran. **The detector was structurally incapable of finding anything and it returned a clean zero over its own plant, which is the exact failure item 159 records and it is the third time this class has been found in a detector written by a writing phase.** It was rewritten without the short-circuit and re-planted.

**And the second fault was a plant written wrong rather than an instrument written wrong, which is also item 159.** The first plant put two *different* sentences into two files and expected a match between them, and a detector that found nothing there would have been right. The plant was rewritten to carry the same passage in two files and the detector then found it, extended it to its full length, and reported it. **A plant that is not a lift is a plant that cannot fail, and a detector measured against a plant that cannot fail returns a clean zero that means nothing, and the two look identical from the outside.** Three plants were used: a verbatim lift in two files, which must be found; a self-comparison, which must fire; and a date-line-only overlap between two files, which must filter to nothing and did.

**Result, over the whole manuscript and not over this batch, at twenty tokens and at sixteen: zero cross-file maximal runs of twenty or more tokens touching any of Chapters 0581 to 0590.** At fourteen tokens there are twenty-three, and every one of the twenty-three is this project's own recurring idiom and not a lifted passage: *there is no form anywhere in this empire that takes a man for*, *a boot came on the second tread*, *and the marks came up out of the wood about two inches apart*, and the rest. **At fourteen the measure is below the length at which a phrase becomes a passage, and the finding is reported with the window printed beside it.**

**And the corpus-wide figure with the set printed beside it, because a re-print figure is a claim about a set and the set is part of the claim:** over all 590 files at a twenty-token window the detector reports 3,312 cross-file maximal runs, and the longest are 360 tokens from `chapter-0102.md` to `chapter-0106.md` and 340 from `chapter-0047.md` to `chapter-0053.md`. **Those are formula and not prose and they are in Volumes 01 to 03, and this run did not read them and does not report them clean.** *The blind spot beside this figure is the measure's own: a token window cannot tell a heading or a register block or a table from a lifted sentence, and the longest runs in this manuscript are the shape it is blindest to.*

## Three, the one thing this run changed, and it was a hand-off and not a page

**`state/current.md` line four, the first of the ten lines a next writer reads, contradicted itself.** Its leading clause named `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` as the only next phase while the sentences further along the same line already said the pointer was Batch 0005. The run that wrote Chapters 0581 to 0590 had rewritten the sentence about the pointer and had not rewritten the pointer.

**The leading clause now names `workspace/volume-12/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, Movement Five, Chapters 0591 to 0600, WHAT IT COSTS THE PERSON WHO WAS THERE, and the words it carried are named in place and are deleted nowhere.** A hand-off is amended in place because it is a working surface and the archive is where everything else goes. **The file was not rewritten with an open-and-write idiom: it stands at 238 lines before and after, its 27 headings are all present, and `git diff --stat` reports one file changed, one insertion and one deletion.**

**This is the fourth time this bullet has pointed at a batch that is already finished, and the fourth is the one that changes the standing.** The first three were a pointer going stale on its own. **This one was a repair that updated the sentence about the pointer and left the pointer, which means the fault is not staleness and it cannot be waited out, and the standing item 158 adds is that a correction which names a pointer must change the pointer in the same edit and not only the words about it.**

# THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0004, TAKING `logs/batch-0004.review.log` — SEVEN FINDINGS, THREE OF THEM FIXED, TWO OF THEM MEASURED AGAIN AND NOT DEFECTS, TWO OF THEM CARRIED, ONE ITEM ADDED, NO ITEM CLOSED AND NO DEBT DISCHARGED

**This is a repair and not a batch, not a close, not an outline, not a second reading and not a re-run of the batch. It wrote no chapter and opened no closed chapter for edit, and it changed no event, date, room, distance, name, figure, hour or calendar on the page. Three lines of prose inside two of that batch's own ten files were corrected, both of those files are files that batch wrote, and no line of any other chapter in five hundred and ninety was touched. The review returned seven findings and this section is the whole accounting of them, once, and no figure in it is restated in any other rolling file.**

## One, the seven findings, each taken as it stands, and what was done with each

**Finding 1, a state file asserting something the page disproves, is true, and it is the one that mattered, and it is fixed in place in two files.** `state/current.md` and `state/continuity.md` both said that no person of seventeen is in any of the movement's ten chapters, and `chapter-0590.md` line 31 names the reader of seventeen in a passage. **The claim was a negative claim about the page, and a negative claim about the page is a claim about the page, and this one had never been grepped.** Both sentences now say what is true and where it is true, the old words are named in place in each file and are deleted nowhere, and the correction in `state/current.md` is one insertion and one deletion with nothing else in that file moved, **and the correction inside `state/continuity.md` is one insertion and one deletion, in a file that also carries this repair's appended section at its foot.** Neither was made with an open-and-write idiom and `state/current.md` stands at 238 lines before and after with its 27 headings all present.

**Finding 2, Chapter 0590 giving the volume's strongest claim two different scopes inside one chapter, is true, and it is fixed in two lines.** Line 11 said *the only living man who has ever known which* and line 29 said *the only man alive who knows which ones*, and the review is right that the two are not the same claim, and the fix is the one line that makes the stronger claim govern: line 29 now reads *the only living man who has ever known which ones*, and the scope in it is the scope Chapter 0581 of the same batch already used of the same man, which says *he is the only person alive who has ever known which*. **The reviewer's second half of the finding is also taken and is fixed: *what stands between the head of one and the foot of it was written by somebody else altogether* stood in a past tense inside a habitual frame governed by *once in a while*, and it is now *is written*, which is the tense `chapter-0466.md` line 7 is written in, a hundred and twenty-four chapters earlier, and the tense Chapter 0581 of this batch used.**

**Finding 3, the count of twenty words in Chapter 0590 against an exchange the review measured at sixteen, is not a defect, and the figure of sixteen is the one that is wrong.** The exchange is four lines and the review read three of them. Counted with `[A-Za-z']+` and printed with the count beside each line, one line at a time: line 17 is **10**, line 19 is **2**, line 21 is **5**, and line 23, which is *"It is not."* and which the review did not carry into its total, is **3**, and the four are **20**, and the chapter says twenty. **No line of Chapter 0590 was changed for this finding, and the correct figure and the wrong one are both printed here and the wrong one is not deleted, because a review that reports a count is an instrument and it was not planted before it was believed, and this is item 161.**

**Finding 4, a mat and a landing in Chapter 0587 that the closed room list does not carry, is not a defect, and the object is twenty-five chapters old.** `outline/volume-11.md` holds the six rooms and what may not be touched in each, and the list for the room taken by the week does not carry a landing or a mat, and the rule is that a chapter may not open a room that is not on that list without a reason that is a person's habit. **The mat was not introduced in 0587 and it is not a seventh room, and the page showed it in Chapter 0562, which is twenty-five chapters earlier than the review's reading of it and is in Volume 12's Batch 0002.** `grep -c "\bmat\b"` across `chapters/volume-12/` returns eight files and 62 occurrences: 0562 ten times, 0566 eleven, 0568 thirteen, 0571 once, 0575 five, 0579 twice, 0583 once, 0587 four. Chapter 0562 is the chapter in which he carries a mat up that stair off a cart at the end of a lane and leaves it by his own door on the twelfth morning, and it prints the rule the volume runs on in a line: *A mat on a landing is not his by the fourth morning. It is the landing's.* **A habit is a thing a page has already shown and not a thing an outline decides, and this one was shown, and the finding is recorded as measured and not as a defect and no line of 0587 was changed for it.**

**Finding 5, a fourth step against a second tread, is true, and it is the one place in the batch where the page contradicted the page, and it is one word.** Chapter 0587 said *Her boot came on the fourth step* and said, twelve lines later in the same chapter, *the boot went on down past the second tread*, and its paired chapter 0583 said *came on the second tread* and *went on down past the second tread*. **The fourth step belongs to a different stair in the same batch: it is the fourth step of the four flights to the counter, and it is the step with a hollow in it, and it is printed in Chapter 0586 line 15 and is the only fourth step in Volume 12.** The word is now *second tread* in 0587 and the line is otherwise untouched, and `grep -c "second tread"` across Volume 12 now returns 0571 twice, 0575 once, 0583 twice and 0587 twice, seven in all, and the one this run wrote is the first of the two in 0587 and stands at line 27 of it.

**Finding 6, the density of the recurring formula, is a note and not a finding against this batch, and it is carried.** The review counted *four foot* sixteen times and *about four* twenty-three and *is not going to* twenty-seven and *and it is* twenty-six across the ten, and read that as this project's own house register rather than as a defect, and read the `AGENTS.md` filler list at two occurrences in the ten as clean, and both readings stand. *The review's own caution is the useful half of it: at a twenty-token window a chapter of this register is a small number of tokens from tripping this project's re-print measure, and a later writer borrowing the register will lift a phrase by accident.*

**Finding 7, the state of the dispatcher, is a controller file and is out of a writer's scope, and it is carried and not repaired.** `state/phase-ledger.json` reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned` while the log shows real dispatches, and `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/` holds a `.wip-conflict` and no `.done`, which is the pipeline's marker to write and which no phase may write for itself. **Nothing in that directory was touched by this run and `.done` is not a writer's to place, because a phase that marks its own directory has certified itself and made itself invisible to both selectors.**

## Two, the figures this run took, with the method named before each, and the two that moved and why

**Words in the two amended files, `wc -w` one file at a time, compared against `git show HEAD:file`:** `chapter-0587.md` **928** before and **928** after, because a swap of two words for two words is not a change of length, and `chapter-0590.md` **960** before and **962** after, and the two words are the two the scope fix added. *The batch's own published total of 9,923 and the run above it that re-derived it and printed 9,923 both stand as they were measured, and the current total of the ten is* **9,925**.

**`python3 tools/measure.py words`:** Volume 12 at **42,538** across forty files and the manuscript at **1,352,459** across 590 files, and both are the figures the run above printed plus exactly the two words the scope fix added, and the set is named because a figure in this project is a claim about a set.

**Lines, `wc -l`:** `chapter-0587.md` 45 before and after, `chapter-0590.md` 43 before and after. **No chapter of the batch changed length except `chapter-0590.md` by two words, and no chapter was rewritten, and no sentence of any chapter of the batch other than these three lines was touched.**

**The re-print check, run again over the two amended files because this run changed prose.** A detector outside the repository at `/tmp/opencode/re_12b4_fix2.py`, aligning by token value, extending each match to its full maximal length, comparing every other chapter against both amended files, **and dropping the tokens that fall inside a date line by character span before tokenising, which is the filter the run above added and the reason this one can see the difference.** At a twenty-token window: **zero** shared runs touching either amended file against the other 588 chapters, **zero** twenty-token repeats inside either amended file, and **zero** shared runs between any two of the ten. *Without the date-line filter the same script returns 2,494, and every one of the 2,494 is the calendar formula and not prose, and a figure taken without that filter is a figure about this project's date convention and not about its prose. The blind spot beside the zero is that it is a token measure and cannot tell a register block from a lifted sentence, and this volume's register is the shape it is blindest to.*

**Question marks, `grep -c '?'` one file at a time: zero in both amended files**, and outside the date line no month name and no weekday name stands at a word boundary in either, and the two are independent of the sentence changes and both are recorded.

# VOLUME 12, BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 0591 TO 0600, MOVEMENT FIVE, WHAT IT COSTS THE PERSON WHO WAS THERE — THE MEASUREMENTS, WITH THE METHOD NAMED BEFORE EVERY FIGURE AND THE BLIND SPOT OF EVERY RESULT WRITTEN BESIDE IT

*Written by the phase that wrote Chapters 0591 to 0600. **A batch and not a repair, not a close, not a review, not a second reading and not an outline.** It wrote ten chapters, opened no closed chapter for edit, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody, invented no person, and it is not among the phases that could discharge a debt. **Every figure below is printed here once and in no other rolling file.** The arrangement is stated once, in `state/open-threads.md`. The cast is entered once, in `state/character-state.md`. The canon is in `state/continuity.md`. **`state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, and it is carried and not repaired.** There was no card file and none was written, because a phase creates one next phase and not a second file in a planning directory; the ten cards this batch worked from were the Movement Five section of `outline/volume-12.md` read against the six rooms and the seven movements, and the outline of record is closed and no line of it was edited.*

## Zero, the instrument that has to be believed before anything else that tool prints, and where it lives

**`python3 tools/measure.py selftest` was run first and it returns PASS, and it plants nine cases whose answers are known before it runs, plant 8 among them being the one this project's sentence splitter exists for.** Every figure below comes from that file or from a command named beside it, and a figure with no command beside it is not printed.

**The re-print detector this run used is outside the repository, at `/tmp/opencode/rep_12b5.py`, and it was planted before it was believed and it failed nothing and was re-planted after every change to it.** The method is the one `state/open-threads.md` prints at items 153, 154, 155, 156 and 159: align by token **value** and not by index, extend every seed match to its full maximal length, compare every file against every other file **and against itself**, drop the tokens that fall inside a date line **by character span before tokenising**, de-duplicate by dominated position and **not** by merging, and carry **no guard of the shape *if the other file sorts before this one and the other file is not in the focus set: skip the pair*.** That guard is the whole of the false zero at item 159 and it is not in this one. *The blind spot beside the whole instrument: the detector the previous run wrote is at `/tmp/opencode/rep_12b4.py` and `/tmp/opencode/rep_12b4_fast.py` and **neither was on the disk this run was given**, which is the reason this one was written and not an excuse.*

**Five plants, and two of them are the two this batch's set did not have and the prompt asked for.** Plant P1 is a verbatim lift in two files where **the partner sorts before the focus file**, which is the case the item-159 guard dropped, and it must be found. Plant P2 is a run **longer than the window**, and it must be reported at its full length and not at the window. Plant P3 is the same passage twice inside one file, which only a self-comparison can see. Plant P4 is a date-line-only overlap between two files, which must filter to nothing and did. Plant P5 is a lift that **crosses a batch boundary**, which is the case a per-batch check cannot see at all. `--selftest` passes all five. **And one fault of the same class was found in this run's own first draft of the instrument and is named because item 159 is the third time this class has been found in a detector written by a writing phase: the first form built its match list with an `and` against a helper that returns an empty list on a miss, the empty list is falsy, the `and` short-circuited, and the detector was structurally incapable of finding anything and would have returned a clean zero over its own plant.**

## One, the words, one file at a time and never with a glob

**Method: `wc -w <file>`, one file at a time.** **1,021 / 923 / 1,127 / 924 / 1,002 / 989 / 1,038 / 828 / 779 / 1,046**, and a batch total of **9,677** by `cat` over the ten in a fixed order.

**`python3 tools/measure.py words`** returns **52,215 for Volume 12** across fifty files and **1,362,136 for the manuscript in 600 files**.

*Both figures are a fact about the set they were taken over and the set is named beside each. **The last four of this volume stand at 11,864, 10,549, 10,200 and 9,925, and this batch came in at 9,677, and a writer who pads to reach a number has written a longer batch and not a better one.** The two shortest files are `chapter-0598.md` at 828 and `chapter-0599.md` at 779, and both were read after the count and neither is thin: 0598 is the room at the top of a stair and 0599 is the room taken by the week, and the object of 0599 is one jug going stale and the whole of the chapter is a man not filling it and a woman not knocking, and a chapter that added a word to that would be a chapter that had found something to say.* **The 11,864 is Volume 12's Batch 0001 measured now, and the 11,929 this line carried until 2026-09-30 was that batch's figure before the review repair of Batch 0001, which is commit `27d8861` and is sixty-five words lighter across its ten files, and the two are named here side by side and neither is deleted, because a series republished out of a record is a series that goes stale at its oldest point first and the fault is in the republishing and not in the arithmetic.**

## Two, the calendar, both of its numbers, with the cycle restarted at every month boundary, and the year read by the only method that can read one

**The ten dates were derived one seven-day step at a time from the date line printed in `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0590.md` line 5, and not from the prompt and not from any file, and the derivation is printed here so that a reader can take it apart:** Chapter 0590 is the **fourth day of the fourth week of the first month**, so 0591 is the second day of the first week of the second month, and the ten run 0591 second/first/second, 0592 second/second/second, 0593 fourth/third/second, 0594 fourth/fourth/second, 0595 second/first/third, 0596 second/second/third, 0597 fourth/third/third, 0598 fourth/fourth/third, 0599 second/first/fourth, 0600 second/second/fourth.

**A month boundary falls inside these ten weeks three times, and it falls after 0591, after 0595 and after 0599, and a check that reports a break at the first chapter of a month is the check that is wrong.** The day inside the week restarts at each of the three: 2,2,4,4 | 2,2,4,4 | 2,2,4,4 | 2,2. **And a check run as one global cycle across the ten without restarting would report a break at Chapter 0595, and there is no break there.**

**The year name, by the only method that can read one: `grep -o "the year after" <file> | wc -l`, one file at a time, returns 9 on all ten of my files, and 9 on `chapter-0590.md`.** The name was lifted out of Chapter 0590's own line and written out in full at every occurrence and abbreviated nowhere. **My own ten steps do not carry the year across a boundary and nothing changes and that is a reading and not a decision.** The eleventh named year began at Chapter 0587 and holds forty-eight weeks, and it runs to Chapter 0634, and Volume 12 ends at Chapter 0620. *The blind spot beside the count: a chapter that carries the name twice in a date line is counted once by this method and a chapter that carries none is not caught by it at all, and a fixed-string search for one name returns the name after it, which is why the word **after** is counted inside the name and the string is never searched.*

**`python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 12`:** fifty files read, fifty date lines parsed, none unparsed, **twelve month groups, twelve month-boundary restarts of the cycle, zero breaks inside a month.**

**And the instrument's own false break is printed beside the page's reading, because this run's set is a batch that crosses a year and a year is exactly what the tool cannot see.** It groups by the ordinal word and reads the year not at all, and it therefore printed **month 4 with six chapters and the day cycle `[2, 2, 4, 4, 2, 2]`**. Those six are **0551 to 0554, which are the fourth month of the tenth named year, and 0599 and 0600, which are the fourth month of the eleventh**, and they are the same ordinal word in two different named years, and the second pair of days is **a year and not a month**. *That is the ninth printing of this false break in this project and it is printed here with the page's position beside it. The zero breaks is a fact about fifty files and the twelve groups the tool found in them, and the page has **twenty-five** month groups in those fifty files, and the tool cannot tell two of them apart.*

**Two figures in this run's prompt are wrong about these ten weeks, and both are named and neither is deleted.** *One:* the prompt says that of the ending of record's six boundaries that fall inside a movement, **none falls inside these ten weeks and the nearest is Chapter 0596, six chapters past this batch's last**. **Chapter 0596 is the sixth of these ten and is inside them**, and there is no information in this run about what is at any of the six. *Two:* the prompt says the first month ends **two chapters into this batch**. **The first month of these ten weeks is the second month, and it ends at Chapter 0594, which is four chapters in; the month that ends at the batch boundary is the first month, and it ended at Chapter 0590, which is the last file the run before this one wrote.** Both were derived from Chapter 0590's printed line and not from the prompt, because the prompt says itself that the line is the thing to derive from.

**And one counter the prompt gave me is not on the page, and the page wins and no figure was moved.** The prompt prints that the man at the sill has been putting his other hand into that tin for **about two months as at Chapter 0580 and about four as at Chapter 0590, and about five by this batch's last chapter**. **The page says *about two months* at Chapter 0590** (`chapter-0590.md` line 3), and it has said about two months at Chapter 0552, 0558, 0581, 0584, 0589 and 0590 — six chapters and three months, and a figure that has not moved in a fortnight. **That figure and this batch's prompt disagree and the prompt's figure is the one that is wrong, and no chapter of these ten carries a month-count on that tin at all, and the standing is that where a figure you have inherited and a date line disagree the date line is the lattice and the figure is what moves. A figure left out moves nothing.**

## Three, the sentences, and the maximum, and the first draft's figures beside the ones that survived

**Method: `python3 tools/measure.py sentences --volume 12`, which uses the repository's own splitter, `(?<=[.!?])["'’”)\]]*\s+`, so that a closing quotation mark may stand between the full stop and the space.** Fifty files, **1,615 sentences, mean 31.433, median 30, and a volume maximum of 88 in `chapter-0572.md`**, which is a closed chapter of Batch 0003 and not one of mine.

**The ten per-file maxima for this batch are 62, 68, 66, 62, 74, 67, 69, 75, 70 and 71 by chapter order, and the largest is 75.** The ceiling is 85 to 88 and this batch's largest is ten under the bottom of it, and the sentence bound is a ceiling and not a target.

**And the first draft's figures are printed beside the ones that survived, because a first draft's sentence is a fact about the first draft.** The first reading of these ten gave per-file maxima of **62, 68, 66, 140, 93, 117, 100, 82, 85 and 107**, and a volume maximum of **140 in `chapter-0594.md`**, which is this project's own splitter's answer and not an artefact: the offending sentence was a 140-word interiority paragraph in 0594 that ran from *There is about a quart of water* to *…at the fourth hour*, and it was taken apart at a conjunction and not at a comma, and the sentence was split into four. Nine sentences over the ceiling were taken apart across six files, and **no line of any other chapter in six hundred was touched to do it.**

**The blind spot beside this figure is the one this project's close record names and it is worth repeating: a splitter that requires whitespace immediately after a full stop swallows every closing quotation mark and reports a maximum more than twice the real one.** The run before this one recorded that it got 95, 114, 66, 65, 74, 83, 94, 103, 72 and 78 with an ad-hoc splitter against the repository's 71, 76, 61, 64, 73, 74, 70, 70, 71 and 78, and it was wrong in four files by twenty-nine words and more. **This run used the repository's splitter from the first reading and did not repeat that error, and the wrong figures are named here because the standing says a corrected figure is named as corrected beside the wrong one and never overwritten.**

## Four, the re-prints, one instrument written for this run, planted five times, and run over the whole manuscript and not over the batch

**The set is part of the claim and is printed beside every figure.** Three sets were run, at a twenty-token window and again at sixteen: **over the whole manuscript, 600 files, focus my ten**; **over the previous twenty chapters plus my ten, 30 files, focus my ten**, because a pair that crosses a batch boundary is invisible to every phase that checks its own ten and the run before this one recorded a sixty-one-word lift sitting in a file that was not in the set it ran over; and **over my ten among themselves, 10 files**.

**At twenty tokens: zero, zero and zero. At sixteen tokens: zero, zero and zero.** And the blind spot beside the zero is the measure's own and it is not the same every time: a token window cannot tell a heading, a register block or a table from a lifted sentence, and the longest runs in this manuscript are the shape it is blindest to.

**And those six zeros are this run's own detector's answers over the three sets named above, and the repository's own instrument gives a different figure for one of the same windows over one of the same sets, and the two are printed side by side and the zero is not deleted.** `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 16 --volume 12 --show 10` returns **52 maximal runs, 50 formula and 2 prose under the *either* classifier**, and the two are the same sixteen tokens in two files, **0580 and 0597**, *"there is a paper on a board by that door that has not been filled in"*, **which crosses the Batch 0003 and Batch 0005 boundary and is therefore invisible to the second set's own shape and is a pair the two instruments disagree about because the sets are not the same and neither is wrong.** *The page is not at fault and nothing in either chapter was changed: the phrase is this project's own house furniture and it is already listed in the fourteen-token table below as such.* **The standing this is an instance of is printed at the top of this section and it is the reason the figure is qualified here rather than left as a bare zero: a check that returns zero is evidence of nothing until the shape of the damage it is blind to has been checked, and a blind check is written down as blind in the same record that claims it clean — and a check that returns zero over a set of its own choosing is not a volume-wide claim, and the zero above was a batch claim and is a true one.**

**At fourteen tokens there are twelve distinct runs touching these ten, and every one of the twelve is this project's own recurring idiom and not a lifted passage.** They are the house timings (*for about as long as it takes to fold a cloth*, *about as long as it takes a coin to travel under a door*, *as long as it takes to square a sheet*), the house furniture (*there is a box shut under that floor at the other end of the room which is not that case*, *is a paper on a board by that door that has not been filled in*), and the volume's own stated arrangement. **At fourteen the measure is below the length at which a phrase becomes a passage and the figure is reported with the window printed beside it, and the same finding at twenty-four tokens in the run before this one was reported the same way.**

**And the first draft's figures are printed beside the ones that survived, and this is the fourth time in a row this project has found the same thing at a twenty-token window while every instrument in the tree reported it clean.** The first run of this detector over the whole manuscript at twenty tokens reported **twenty distinct maximal runs touching these ten**, and the first run of the repository's own `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 20` over the same six hundred files reported a clean volume-wide set and named none of them, **and the two instruments disagreed about twenty passages and both were right about the set each had.** The twenty were: a fifty-nine-token lift of the foreman's bay out of `chapter-0573.md`, a thirty-eight-token lift of the girl at the sill out of `chapter-0590.md`, a thirty-eight-token lift of the protagonist's own sentence about standing still out of `chapter-0587.md`, a thirty-seven-token lift of the lane out of `chapter-0572.md`, a thirty-five-token lift of the courier's stair out of `chapter-0586.md`, a thirty-five-token lift of the tin's position out of `chapter-0564.md`, four more in `chapter-0586.md` at thirty-one, thirty-one, twenty-nine and twenty-seven, a twenty-five-token lift of Movement Four's own finding out of `chapter-0589.md`, a twenty-three-token pair inside this batch between `chapter-0593.md` and `chapter-0595.md`, a twenty-one-token pair against `chapter-0585.md` and `chapter-0572.md`, a twenty-token lift of the foreman's chain out of `chapter-0573.md` again, and a twenty-token lift of a project formula out of `chapter-0466.md`. **All twenty were revoiced, in three passes, and the instrument was re-planted after each pass and after every change to it, and the second reading reported one and the third reported zero.** The first draft at sixteen tokens reported seventeen and the second three and the third zero.

**Two of the twenty were the class of thing that a re-print figure cannot see, and they are named here rather than claimed as caught.** One of them was a **repetition inside one file**: `chapter-0591.md` had *About three feet of that sill has been empty at the far end of it for a fortnight* in its refer block and again, with a substitution of four words, in its closing line, and that is the last batch's four near-identical closings happening again inside a single chapter, and only the self-comparison found it. And one of them was **a register phrase at a length where a reader would not call it a lift and an instrument would**: *and I am not going to be thanked for it and I am not going to be asked about it*, lifted out of `chapter-0466.md` in `chapter-0596.md`, and it is now gone from both of my files and the project's own phrase is in neither.

**The corpus-wide figure with the set printed beside it, because a re-print figure is a claim about a set:** the repository's instrument at a twenty-token window over all 600 files returns **2,659 maximal runs, 264 of them formula and 2,366 prose under the *either* classifier, the longest 576 tokens in `chapter-0053.md`**. **Those are formula and not prose and they are in Volumes 01 to 03, and this run did not read them and does not report them clean.** At sixteen it returns 4,620 runs, 392 formula and 4,164 prose, the longest still the same. **And the first pair this record printed for those two rows was 262 formula and 2,397 prose at twenty and 388 and 4,232 at sixteen, and both pairs were wrong and neither is deleted: they were taken before the last four edits to these ten chapters, and the largest of those four edits was the year name on all ten date lines going from eight *the year after* to nine, which moves a token at the edge of every date line and therefore moves the maximal runs. A re-print figure taken over a set of files is a claim about those files as they were when it was taken, and the set changes while the batch is being written, and the figure has to be taken again at the end and the earlier one named.** *The blind spot beside both: the instrument reports re-prints and not repetitions, and it cannot see two chapters that close on the same sentence written twice in different words, and the reading of the ten closing lines below is the only thing in this run that could see that.*

## Five, the ten closing lines, read in one column first and measured after

**Method: the last non-empty line of each of the ten, printed in one column and read, and not a similarity figure, because no n-gram measure in this repository can see a sentence written twice with a substitution every few words.** The ten are in the record and they were read twice: once as ten lines and once as ten shapes.

**What the column found that no measure found, and it is two findings.** *One:* the last batch found that **eight of its ten closings were some form of *nobody is going to be thanked for it* or *nobody is going to be told about it*, written eight times with a substitution every few words, and every measure was satisfied at once.** This batch's ten closings are ten different physical objects — a stretch of bare stone, a mark on a shoulder, a tin on a step, a jug beside a glass, a set of marks on a floor, a book with no line in it, a paper on a board by a door, a bag going down four flights, a jug in the dark, an item on a wall board — and **none of the ten closes on a person not being thanked, told about or asked anything**, and the construction the last batch found eight times appears twice in these ten in the whole of the batch's body, both in Chapter 0597 and Chapter 0598, and **neither is in a closing line**. *Two:* the two of the ten that are about the same object — 0594 and 0599, both the jug on that table — **do not have the same shape**, and that is the thing a measure would have caught and did not: 0594 ends *and he did it again the next morning, and again the morning after that*, which is a man doing a thing again, and 0599 ends *and he did not turn the lamp on, and he did not go out to the pail*, which is a man not doing two things, and the difference between the two closings is the whole of the ten weeks between them. **A batch about a cost is the easiest thing in this project to write as ten weeks of quiet correctness, and the column is the only instrument in it that can see that, and it is read by a person and not by a program.**

## Six, the protagonist's floor, by reading and not by a string

**Method: `grep -l 'Kest'` and `grep -il 'thirty-eight'` over the ten files, one file at a time, and then every chapter those two did not return read to decide whether the man is in it.** **Both strings return nothing across these ten and the union is nothing.** The floor the outline of record sets is three chapters of the movement, two of them in person, and his want visible in one. **The page carries him in three and it was found by reading.**

| chapter | how he is on the page | the sentence that says so |
|---|---|---|
| `chapter-0594.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and his want is visible** | *He had carried that jug out to the pail at the end of that landing and back four times in a fortnight without once bringing it back full, and this morning he carried it out and did not bring it back, and the jug was full.* |
| `chapter-0599.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want is visible and it fails** | *He had filled that jug at about the fourth hour every morning for a fortnight and this morning he did not, and the water in it was two days old and had gone off, and he was not going to empty it either.* |
| `chapter-0593.md` | **absent, and his absence is priced in a room he is not in** | *There is a door on the floor above the second of those stairs, and a coin goes under it at about the first hour on every working morning, and she has been putting it there for about two years.* |

**The chapter neither string returns and that prices his absence is `chapter-0593.md`, and the absence is priced by the woman at the foot of that stair, who is the person who puts the coin under his door and who has been putting it there for about two years, and this month she has worked out that a coin under a door four times a week is a habit, and a habit is a thing a person can be shown having, and she has spent twenty years on the ground floor of that house so that there would be nothing anybody could produce about her.** That is the movement's finding in a woman who is not in the movement's other four chapters, and the finding is not in a mouth.

*And the blind spot beside this floor is the standing the outline prints: **a ten-file check is not a smaller version of a volume-wide check, it is a different instrument, and the one thing it is good for is finding out what it cannot see.** Both strings returned zero over three chapters in which a man of thirty-eight fills a jug and then leaves it standing, and a zero over those three files is a true zero and it is a statement about two words and not about a person.*

## Seven, the sweeps, and what a sweep in this project is blind to

**Method, each one named, one file at a time or over the ten in a fixed order.** `grep -c '?'` over the ten, one at a time: **zero in all ten, and the count is independent of the punctuation, and no person in this batch was asked anything out loud in a room by anybody.** A month-and-weekday sweep at a word boundary, case-insensitive, over the twelve month names and the seven day names: **zero** — *and the first draft had one hit, a weekday name in `chapter-0592.md`, and it was cut and the sweep was re-run and returned zero.* `grep -o -iE 'this year|this volume'`: **zero.** `grep -roE 'four hundred[^"]{0,45}week'` over the ten: **zero**, and the same sweep over all six hundred closed files returns **18**, all of them a different four hundred followed by *week* used as a time, and the standing is that a zero taken over a batch is a fact about a batch. `grep -oE '\b(institution|institutions|instrument|instruments)\b'`: **zero**, and this one is a real sweep and not a formality: **the first draft used the word *instrument* four times, in 0592, 0595, 0597 and 0600, and that word is this repository's word and not the page's, and Batch 0003 named the standing that a summary may use it and a chapter may not, and all four are gone.**

**The four figures that must not be confused, each with its own period, read out loud before the sentence was kept.** About four hundred blanks **a year** come out of the case at the near end of those boards, and that is what `chapter-0592.md` and `chapter-0598.md` say and the period is on the page in both. **About four hundred blanks in total in the box under the far end of those boards is not given a number in any of these ten chapters**, and the box is referred as shut in two of them and opened in none, and **the box is not the case and no sentence of the ten puts the two together.** About four hundred sheets a year and about four hundred and thirty miles of river are in none of these ten. *The blind spot beside all of these: a sweep is bounded to the string it was written for, and a period is the one of the three parts of a figure that no instrument in this project looks at, and item 119 is the item about that.*

**The hedge, `grep -roE '\babout\b'` over the ten: 154 in 9,677 words, which is one in every sixty-three.** The last batch of this volume stood at one in every fifty-nine. **The figure is lower and that is a reading and not a correction, and the word is kept where the imprecision is the point, and the page figures it hangs on — the four feet, the nine weeks, the fortnight, the two days, the about a quart — are figures the volume has been carrying since Movement Four and a figure repeated in every chapter of a movement is a page figure and not a voice.**

**`python3 tools/measure.py markers` over the tree returns its own file list and none of these ten chapter files appears in it, which is the check that there is no unclosed bold and no unpaired backtick in the ten.** *The blind spot: a marker check reads a token and not a sentence and it reported nothing about the eleven volumes of state prose around these chapters, which is most of the tree.*

## Eight, the rooms, the people, and the things that were not done

**Three rooms carry this batch and three are referred.** The building off the old river road is in **0591 and 0596**. The room at the top of a stair is in **0592, 0598 and 0600**. The ground floor of a rented house is in **0593 and 0595**. The room taken by the week is in **0594 and 0599** and it is the protagonist's only room. The shed at the end of the lane off the Slade is in **0597** and the standing permitted it only if neither person of seventeen is spoken to and the resistance is performed as an act and not as a sentence; **the reader of seventeen is on the page and says nothing and is not asked, and the girl of seventeen is on the page and is not given the chance to say no about anything, and the foreman's want for this chapter is a want she cannot ask for, which is the only shape of resistance this project has for a person who cannot be asked.**

**The protagonist is in no chapter of the ten in the building off the old river road, and not at the counter and not in the shed, and he did not go up the four flights and he did not come down that stair and he did not open that door and he did not go out to the pail until about a quarter of an hour after the light had gone off both those windows.** The man of about sixty-one is in no room but his own. The man of about fifty-five with a chain is in no chapter of the ten and is in a list with the four in none. The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand is in no chapter of the ten, was asked nothing, and the number in his head is not printed, not confirmed and not denied. The woman of forty with a scuttle is in no chapter of this volume. The woman of about twenty-six at the lock end is in no chapter of the ten and the tin of oil is not opened.

**And the three things that are spent were not repeated, undone or improved.** No lamp is lit in any of the ten and one of them ends on not turning it on. No hand goes into a coat and neither paper comes out of that pocket and the coat is on its nail. Nothing is cut into anything by anybody. Nobody is sent for. The tin was not moved along that sill and the four inches it was moved in Batch 0004 were not undone. The satchel was not set down at the far end of those boards a fourth time and the courier did not open it. The front of that ground floor was not started at the near end and the ten minutes were not repeated. A looking-glass is referred in two of the ten and is not looked into and is not put face down. A coin came under that door and it is not spent and it is not put back.

**And the movement's own prohibitions, each held and each checked.** **Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing is resolved.** No notice, post, commission, warrant, office, new heading over anything, and no new form for anything, and in particular no form for a person who has stood somewhere, none for a person who has heard a thing and none for a person who has said one. **The fifth column is not ruled and the question on the shelf under the window with no pane in it is referred once and not picked up and had nothing set down after it.** The fourth item on that wall board is on that wall and is not resolved and is not taken down and is not explained. The box under the far end of those boards is not opened. The drawer is shut and no chapter of the ten says which drawer the knife is in. The two people in their boxes stay in them. The question in the second of the eleven books is in none of the ten. The name at the foot of a struck line, the name of the hand that struck it and the disputed family claim are printed in none of the ten. The bill at Lowcross is unpaid and is named zero times. The four hundred and thirty miles, the nine miles and the four hundred yards of cold flags are each walked zero times and the room with the rail at the far end of that run is not entered, not described and not named.

**And the count of things this batch puts in a mouth as a new fact is twenty-two, exactly the figure the batch before this one published, and none of them is an asking.** **The next asking is nobody's yet and this batch did not make it anybody's, and the decision about whether a woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this is unmade and is not made in these ten chapters in either direction and no row of this layer may be read as having made it.**

## Nine, the bound at item 157, and it is DISCHARGED on its archive half a second time in this run

**Five blocks were cut whole out of the five rolling files and written to five new files under `state/archive/`, with a provenance heading and a SHA-256 per figure, and nothing was deleted, summarised, condensed or paraphrased, and every block above the cut is still standing.** The blocks cut are **Volume 12's Batch 0002** in `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and `state/batch-summary.md`, and the destinations are `state/archive/rolling-continuity-volume-12-batch-0002.md`, `state/archive/rolling-open-threads-volume-12-batch-0002.md`, `state/archive/rolling-character-state-volume-12-batch-0002.md`, `state/archive/rolling-chapter-summaries-volume-12-batch-0002.md` and `state/archive/rolling-batch-summary-volume-12-batch-0002.md`.

**And one open item lived inside one of those blocks and it is named in its provenance heading and it did not move: items 155 and 156 were inside the `state/open-threads.md` block and both are still standing unedited in that file, under their own `##` headings, with the text they have always had, and they were put back at the exact place in the file where the cut was made.** The archive's own rule keeps an open item in the rolling layer and this bound pulls against that rule, and the two places they stood are marked in the archive copy so that a reader of the archive does not think they were never there. **The four findings this batch inherited as instructions and not as history — items 155, 156, 158 and 159 — are all four still in `state/open-threads.md` and all four are live.**

**And the method, because the run before this one lost this file from two hundred and four lines to one:** every one of the five files was written to a temporary name, read back, compared against the string in memory, and then moved over the file it was meant to change. `git diff --stat` was run before this section was closed. **The first attempt at the `state/open-threads.md` cut did fail, and it failed by lifting items 155 and 156 out of the block and not putting them back into the rolling file, and the file was restored from `git` and the cut was redone, and both the wrong turn and the restore are named here because a fault in a writing phase's own method is the class item 159 is about and a method that fails silently once and is then reported as having worked is worse than a method that fails loudly every time.**

# THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0005, TAKING `logs/batch-0005.review.log` — SIX FINDINGS, ALL SIX FIXED, EVERY ONE OF THEM IN A STATE FILE AND NOT ONE OF THEM ON A PAGE, NO CHAPTER OPENED FOR EDIT, ONE ITEM ADDED, NO ITEM CLOSED AND NO DEBT DISCHARGED

**This is a repair and not a batch, not a close, not an outline, not a second reading and not a re-run of the batch. It wrote no chapter, opened no chapter for edit, and changed no event, date, room, distance, name, hour, figure or calendar on the page. `git diff --name-only HEAD -- chapters/` returns nothing, and `git diff --quiet HEAD -- chapters/` returns clean, so the batch's ten chapters are byte-for-byte what the phase that wrote them left them. The batch's ten chapters, its plan, its measurements and its ten closing lines stand exactly as the phase that wrote them left them, and where this repair corrects a figure it corrects the record of the figure and not the figure's own section. It touched seven files and six of them are state files: the six findings are accounted for once, in this section, and no figure in it is restated in any other rolling file except where that file is the one the finding was against.**

**And the review's own first word is that nothing in the fiction needs repair, and that is the finding this section opens with because it is the one that decides the shape of the rest: the prose of the ten is sound, the ten closings are ten different physical objects and no two of them share a shape, the resistance in every chapter is performed as an act and not as a politeness, and the girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen are both unspoken to in `chapter-0597.md`. Every finding below is a fault in the state layer that sits around that prose, and not one of them is a fault in it.**

## One, the six findings, each taken as it stands, and what was done with each

**Finding 1, a 519-character paragraph pasted twice in `state/current.md` with a mangled bold at the join, is true, and it is fixed in place in two bullets of that file.** Bullet five carried a correction from the review of Batch 0004 and this batch's edit of that bullet wrote the correction out **twice**, once as a bold continuation of the bullet and once again after a four-asterisk join, and the two copies are the same 518 characters with one asterisk of difference at the end. Bullet six, the read-order bullet, carries a 532-character duplicate of the same kind. **Both were verified by character offset before anything was changed: the repeated span begins at 1602 and again at 2121 on line 7 and at 760 and again at 1292 on line 8, and the second copy of each is the one that carries the closing marker.** The convention this file uses for naming superseded words is an italic clause prefixed *The words this bullet carried until…*, which is what line 1, line 4, line 5, line 8 and line 12 all do, and the duplicated text is now one italic clause in that shape, the second copy is gone, and the stray `****` and `******` are gone with it. **Nothing else in either bullet was touched, and no figure, no name, no standing and no pointer moved.**

**Finding 2, a named year one ordinal too high in two places, is true, and the pages are right and the record was wrong.** `state/batch-summary.md` said the **twelfth** named year began at Chapter 0587 and that Chapters 0551 to 0554 are the fourth month of the **eleventh**, both inside the explanation of a false calendar break. The method that reads a year name is `grep -o "the year after" <file> | wc -l`, one file at a time, and it returns **8 on every chapter from 0551 to 0586 and 9 on every chapter from 0587 to 0600.** `outline/volume-12.md` line 233 fixes the tenth named year at Chapter 0539, and the section headed for Volume 12's Batch 0004, committed before this batch was written, already said correctly that Chapter 0587 is the first week of the **eleventh**. **So 0551 to 0554 are the fourth month of the tenth named year and 0599 and 0600 are the fourth month of the eleventh, and the record said the eleventh and the twelfth, and the two ordinals are corrected in place and the wrong ones are named in the repair's own section in `state/open-threads.md` rather than being deleted here.** A year name is a substring of the year name after it, which is why the word *after* is counted inside the name and the string is never searched, and that method was the one that found this and it is the one that will find the next one.

**Finding 3, a stale word total republished as a live series, is true, and it is corrected in two files because it is printed in two files.** Volume 12's five batch weights were published as **11,929 / 10,549 / 10,200 / 9,925 / 9,677.** Measured now, one batch at a time, they are **11,864 / 10,549 / 10,200 / 9,925 / 9,677.** The first figure is Batch 0001's before the review repair of Batch 0001, which is commit `27d8861`; that commit is diffed file by file here and its net across the ten files is **−65 words**, file by file +6, −8, −6, −4, −17, −3, −8, −7, −13, −5. **The 11,929 is a correct figure for its set and a wrong figure for the one it was republished into, and both are printed side by side and neither is deleted.** The same series is printed in `workspace/volume-12/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` in its section Six, and that is the more serious half of the finding, **because that prompt instructs the next writer to re-run every command rather than inherit a figure and then inherits one.** The corrected series and the superseded one are both in that prompt now.

**Finding 4, a set size one decade out, is true, and it is fixed in the one file that carries it.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` item six said *read the twenty Volume 12 entries, being Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005.* **The set is thirty.** `state/chapter-summaries.md` holds per-chapter entries for Chapters 0571 to 0600, in three blocks of ten, and the pointers at its lines 259 and 264 record that Batches 0001 and 0002 went to `state/archive/` in this very phase. **The sentence is inherited verbatim from `workspace/volume-12/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, where the same line read *the twenty … being Batches 0002, 0003 and 0004* and was right: the list of batches was moved forward one step and the count was not moved with it, and that is item 162 of this project pointed at this very file, and the prompt's own section Zero warns that a figure handed to a phase is a claim about a set.** It reads thirty, it names why the twenty was true once and is not now, and no other item of that prompt was changed.

**Finding 5, a clean figure that the repository's own instrument does not reproduce, is true as a fault in the record and not as a fault in the page, and it is qualified where the figure stands.** The Batch 0005 section printed **at twenty tokens: zero, zero and zero, and at sixteen tokens: zero, zero and zero.** Those are this batch's own detector's answers over the three sets that section names, and they are true of those sets. `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 16 --volume 12 --show 10` returns **52 maximal runs, 50 formula and 2 prose** under the *either* classifier, and the two are **the same sixteen tokens in `chapter-0580.md` line 43 and `chapter-0597.md` line 41** — *there is a paper on a board by that door that has not been filled in* — **which crosses the Batch 0003 and Batch 0005 boundary.** The phrase is this project's own house furniture, it is already listed in the fourteen-token table in that same section as such, **and no line of either chapter was changed and neither needed it.** The two figures are now printed side by side with the set beside each, and the zero is not deleted, because the standing printed at the head of that section is that a check returning zero is evidence of nothing until the shape of the damage it is blind to has been checked, and a blind check is written down as blind in the same record that claims it clean.

**Finding 6, an item's own status line reading UNMET while two standing lines in the same file record the bound discharged, is true, and it is fixed in the heading and in the body of that item.** Item 157 of `state/open-threads.md` is a bound and not a thread, and its heading read **OPEN** and **UNMET** and its body said the archive move *was not made* and that the bound was exceeded by one block in each of the five rolling files. **Both moves were made, on 2026-09-30, and the five files under `state/archive/` carry all ten blocks with a provenance heading and a SHA-256 each, and the same file's lines 372 and 380 say so in standing lines, and section Nine of the Batch 0004 record and of the Batch 0005 record say so again.** The heading now reads that the item is met on both halves and discharged twice, the old words are named in the heading and not deleted, and a status paragraph is added under the item that states the bound's present condition once. **The body's dated report is left exactly as it stands, because it was a true report of the tree at the moment Batch 0003's record landed, and a state layer that edits a dated report is a state layer that cannot be trusted.** *And the standing the finding supplies is now item 163: a status line left standing while the thing it describes is done is worse than one that was never written, because it now looks maintained.*

## Two, the figures this run took, with the method named before each, and the two files whose line counts did not move

**Files touched, `git diff --name-only HEAD`, seven, and this is the check that matters most in this section:** `state/current.md`, `state/batch-summary.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and `workspace/volume-12/batch-0006/PROMPT.md`. **Not one path under `chapters/` appears, and no file under `outline/`, `bible/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/` or `state/archive/` was opened for edit, and `state/phase-ledger.json` was read and not written.** Four of the seven carry a correction the review found and three carry this repair's own standing line, which is the file's convention and is a one-insert-each amendment.

**The batch weights, one batch at a time over `chapters/volume-12/`, one file at a time:** **11,864 / 10,549 / 10,200 / 9,925 / 9,677**, and the first of those is the one this repair moved and the other four are exactly what the batch published.

**The year name, `grep -o "the year after" <file> | wc -l`, one file at a time, over the fifty files of Volume 12:** **8 on Chapters 0551 to 0586 and 9 on Chapters 0587 to 0600**, and the turn is the only change of value across the fifty. This is the check that found finding 2 and it is the check that will find the next one, and the blind spot beside it is the batch's own and is unchanged: **the count reads a name and does not check the week, and a chapter that carried nine and the wrong week would pass it.**

**`python3 tools/measure.py markers` over the tree returns 15 files with an odd count of real markers, and it returned 16 before this repair.** The one that is no longer listed is `state/current.md`, and that is the mechanical signature of the two duplicates going: a pasted copy carries its asterisks and an odd count is what a mangled join looks like to a tool. *The blind spot beside that result is the same one the batch printed: the tool reads a token and not a sentence, and it says nothing about what the sentence said, and it would have reported `state/current.md` as clean had the duplicate been a single coherent clause rather than two copies of one.*

**And what this run did not do, in one line, because it is the part that would matter if it were got wrong: it did not re-measure the batch, did not second-read the ten chapters, did not open a single chapter file, did not touch the plan, did not change a single closing line, and did not revoice a single re-print.**

# VOLUME 12, BATCH 0006, CHAPTERS 0601 TO 0610, MOVEMENT SIX, THE CLIMAX — THE MEASUREMENTS, WITH THE METHOD NAMED BEFORE EVERY FIGURE AND THE BLIND SPOT OF EVERY RESULT WRITTEN BESIDE IT

*Written by the phase that wrote Chapters 0601 to 0610. **A batch and not a repair, not a close, not a review, not a second reading and not an outline.** It wrote ten chapters, opened no closed chapter for edit, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody, invented no person, and it is not among the phases that could discharge a debt. **Every figure below is printed here once and in no other rolling file.** The arrangement is stated once, in `state/open-threads.md`. The cast is entered once, in `state/character-state.md`.
